**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.6 (what cannot be concluded from an audit); Google SRE Book, chapter "Effective Troubleshooting" (https://sre.google/sre-book/effective-troubleshooting/)

</details>

**095.** Did the PCI change in row 2 cause a rise in dropped sessions in cluster MK-03?

1. Yes; the difference between PCI 515 and the plan caused the drops.
2. No; no active alarms means that no sessions have been dropped.
3. Yes; the PCI change came from the same batch as the tilt error.
4. Unknown; correlate drops and check PCI 515 collision or confusion.

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　4

**詳解**　The audit shows a difference and its time, but nothing about the KPI. Option 1 equates a difference with an impact. Option 2 is wrong: parameter errors do not raise equipment alarms. Option 3 is guilt by association; each difference must be checked against the data.

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.6 (audit does not prove causation; compare with KPI timing); 3GPP TS 28.541 V18.15.0 clause 4.4.1 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.541/)

</details>

**情境 020**　Timeline of two changes and a re-establishment surge

Cell HB-0507-3 (fictional) shows a surge of re-establishments in its neighbour HB-0511-1 on 2026-11-18. The NOC lines up hourly counters and the changes of that day.

| Hour (11-18) | MM.HoExeInterReq HB-0507-3 → HB-0511-1 | Re-establishments in HB-0511-1 by UEs from HB-0507-3 (vendor counter, fictional) |
|---|---|---|
| 12:00 | 118 | 6 |
| 13:00 | 86 | 31 |
| 14:00 | 0 | 97 |
| 15:00 | 0 | 104 |
| 16:00 | 0 | 99 |
| 20:00 | 0 | 93 |
| 21:00 | 0 | 96 |
| 22:00 | 0 | 95 |
| CHG-A 13:42: NRCellRelation HB-0507-3 → HB-0511-1 isHOAllowed TRUE → FALSE (no approved request found) | | |
| CHG-B 21:10: NRSectorCarrier=HB-0507-3 configuredMaxTxPower 20000 → 40000 (restores planned 40000) | | |
| Alarm list for HB-0507 / HB-0511: not retrieved | | |

**096.** Which change can be excluded as the cause of the onset of the surge?

1. CHG-A, because it has no approved request
2. CHG-B: it came seven hours after the surge began.
3. Neither, because both changes affect HB-0507-3
4. CHG-A, because isHOAllowed does not affect handovers

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　2

**詳解**　A cause cannot come after its effect: re-establishments were already 97–104 per hour from 14:00, and CHG-B came at 21:10 (with no visible change in the 21:00–22:00 values). Option 1 confuses missing paperwork with causal timing. Option 3 ignores the time order. Option 4 is wrong: isHOAllowed FALSE prohibits handover.

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.6 (compare change time with KPI change time); Curriculum 6.2.1; Google SRE Book, chapter "Effective Troubleshooting" (https://sre.google/sre-book/effective-troubleshooting/)

</details>

**097.** Which change is best aligned with the data?

1. CHG-B, because power changes always increase re-establishments
2. Neither: the surge started at 12:00
3. CHG-A: HO attempts fell to zero while re-establishments rose.
4. CHG-A and CHG-B equally, because both happened on 11-18

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　3

**詳解**　isHOAllowed FALSE stops handovers on this relation, so UEs that need HB-0511-1 lose the connection and re-establish there; the partial hour 13:00 and the zero attempts from 14:00 fit a change at 13:42. Option 1 states an invented general rule and conflicts with the timing. Option 2 is wrong: 6 at 12:00 is the baseline. Option 4 ignores the hour-level timing.

**出處**　3GPP TS 28.541 V18.15.0 clause 4.4.1 (isHOAllowed) (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.541/); 3GPP TS 28.552 V18.12.0 clause 5.1.1.6.1.7 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.552/)

</details>

**098.** Compute the mean hourly re-establishments for 14:00–16:00 and the factor relative to the 12:00 value.

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　Mean 100 per hour; about 16.7 times the 12:00 value of 6.

**詳解**　(97 + 104 + 99) / 3 = 300 / 3 = 100; 100 / 6 = 16.67.

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.6 (KPI change around the change time); Curriculum 6.2.1

</details>

**099.** Can a hardware fault in HB-0511-1 be excluded from these data?

1. No; check the alarm histories and equipment status of both sites.
2. Yes; the coincidence with CHG-A rules out a fault on HB-0511-1.
3. Yes; successful re-establishments on HB-0511-1 rule out hardware.
4. No; a re-establishment on the cell always indicates faulty hardware.

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　1

**詳解**　The curriculum asks to exclude hardware faults (6.1.7) first, which needs the alarm and equipment data; the table says "not retrieved". Option 2 uses one supporting fact to dismiss another hypothesis without checking it. Option 3 does not show the absence of a partial hardware fault. Option 4 is an invented rule.

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.6 (exclude 6.1.7 hardware faults first); Curriculum 6.1.7; 3GPP TS 32.111-1 V18.0.0 (alarm handling) (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/32_series/32.111-1/)

</details>

**100.** The analyst wants to set isHOAllowed on the relation HB-0507-3 → HB-0511-1 back to TRUE immediately. What must happen first?

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　The revert is a service-affecting parameter change, so it needs engineer approval through the change process (6.3.2); only read-only checks such as configuration export, comparison and KPI or alarm queries may be done without approval.

**詳解**　The curriculum classifies comparing configurations as read-only verification (6.3.1) and states that reverting parameters goes through the 6.3.2 approval process and the change process of Chapter 9.

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.6 (relation to 6.3.1 and 6.3.2); Curriculum 6.3.2; 3GPP TS 28.541 V18.15.0 clause 4.4.1 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.541/)

</details>

**情境 021**　Night-shift note proposing to roll back a batch

A night-shift engineer at Telco-X (fictional) left the following note after a batch import.

```
Shift handover note 2026-11-25 06:45 (fictional)
- 01:10 batch IMP-1124-02 applied: 37 attribute changes on 12 cells (cluster RV-07)
- From 02:00, complaints and KPI drop on 3 of the 12 cells (cell names in ticket TT-1125-011)
- No equipment alarms on RV-07 sites
- Planning DB for RV-07 last synchronised 2026-11-01
- Change requests approved for RV-07 since 11-01: list not attached
- Proposed actions:
  (a) export live configuration of RV-07
  (b) diff it against the planning DB
  (c) pull change records for IMP-1124-02
  (d) roll back the whole batch with the vendor rollback file
  (e) lock the worst cell until morning to stop complaints
- Per-attribute diff and per-cell KPI: not prepared yet
```

**101.** Which of the following is a read-only verification action that can be done before any approval?

1. Rolling back the batch with the rollback file
2. Locking a cell to stop complaints
3. Setting configuredMaxTxPower back to a previous value
4. Export and diff live config against plan.

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　4

**詳解**　Exporting and comparing configuration does not affect service and is part of the read-only verification of 6.3.1. Options 1, 2 and 3 change the network or its service and need engineer approval (6.3.2).

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.6 (comparing configuration is read-only verification); Curriculum 6.3.1; Curriculum 6.3.2; 3GPP TS 28.541 V18.15.0 clause 4.4.1 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.541/)

</details>

**102.** Why is rolling back the whole batch (d) risky without further checks?

1. Because rollback files can only be applied by the ANR function
2. Because the batch changed fewer than 50 attributes
3. Approved changes since 11-01 are missing.
4. Because no equipment alarms are active

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　3

**詳解**　Without the list of approved requests since 11-01, a blanket rollback could remove intended changes and create new deviations; each change should be classified first. Option 1 is invented. Option 2 is irrelevant. Option 4 is expected for parameter errors and says nothing about rollback risk.

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.6 (planning DB may lag behind approved plan changes); Curriculum 6.3.2; 3GPP TS 28.541 V18.15.0 clause 4.4.1 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.541/)

</details>

**103.** Write the ticket entry for TT-1125-011 that hands the case to the day shift. List the required points.

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　Required points:
- Event: batch IMP-1124-02 applied at 01:10 on 2026-11-25 (37 attribute changes, 12 cells, cluster RV-07)
- Impact: complaints and KPI drop on 3 cells from 02:00 (named in the ticket); no equipment alarms
- Done so far: nothing changed in the network; read-only steps (a)–(c) proposed but not yet done
- Open items: per-attribute diff, per-cell KPI timing, list of change requests approved since 11-01
- Proposed actions (d) rollback and (e) lock are service-affecting and pending engineer approval

**詳解**　The entry records facts, the state of verification and the pending decisions, separating what has been done from what is only proposed.

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.6; Curriculum 6.3.2; Curriculum 6.4.3; 3GPP TS 28.541 V18.15.0 clause 4.4.1 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.541/)

</details>

**104.** According to the curriculum, which pattern is typical of a parameter misconfiguration rather than a hardware fault?

1. An equipmentAlarm on the radio unit followed by loss of the cell
2. No equipment alarm; KPIs worsen after a configuration change, activation or import.
3. All cells of a region degrade at the same time together with an NG link alarm
4. The cell degrades gradually over months without any change records

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　2

**詳解**　The curriculum describes parameter errors as "hardware normal, no equipment alarm, but KPIs worse after a point in time", with that time often matching a parameter change, new site or batch import. Option 1 points to hardware failure (6.1.7). Option 3 points to transport or core problems (6.1.8). Option 4 lacks the change-related onset.

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.6 (characteristics of parameter misconfiguration); Curriculum 6.1.7; Curriculum 6.1.8

</details>

**105.** Which of the 37 changes caused the KPI drop on the 3 cells?

<details><summary>看答案</summary>

**答案**　Cannot be determined: the per-attribute diff and the per-cell KPI series are not prepared yet; the list of changed attributes per cell (with old and new values) and the KPI time series of the 3 affected cells are needed.

**詳解**　The note gives only counts (37 changes, 12 cells, 3 affected). Linking a specific change to the degradation requires knowing which attributes changed on or around the 3 cells and how their KPIs moved from 02:00.

**出處**　Curriculum 6.1.6 (comparison steps: list differences, then compare with KPI timing); 3GPP TS 28.541 V18.15.0 clause 4.4.1 (https://www.3gpp.org/ftp/Specs/archive/28_series/28.541/)

</details>

### GSMA 練習題（81 題）

**1.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**5.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.

C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.

C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.

- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**18.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**22.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**26.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**30.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**39.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**48.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**52.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**57.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**66.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**75.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.

**79.**

Analyze the 5G wireless network drive-test user plane data and engineering parameters.
Identify the reason for the throughput dropping below 600Mbps in certain road sections.
From the following 8 potential root causes, select the most likely one and enclose its number in \boxed{} in the final answer.

C1: The serving cell's downtilt angle is too large, causing weak coverage at the far end.
C2: The serving cell's coverage distance exceeds 1km, resulting in over-shooting.
C3: A neighboring cell provides higher throughput.
C4: Non-colocated co-frequency neighboring cells cause severe overlapping coverage.
C5: Frequent handovers degrade performance.
C6: Neighbor cell and serving cell have the same PCI mod 30, leading to interference.
C7: Test vehicle speed exceeds 40km/h, impacting user throughput.
C8: Average scheduled RBs are below 160, affecting throughput.

Given:
- The default electronic downtilt value is 255, representing a downtilt angle of 6 degrees. Other values represent the actual downtilt angle in degrees.

Beam Scenario and Vertical Beamwidth Relationships:
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to Default or SCENARIO_1 to SCENARIO_5, the vertical beamwidth is 6 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_6 to SCENARIO_11, the vertical beamwidth is 12 degrees.
- When the cell's Beam Scenario is set to SCENARIO_12 or above, the vertical beamwidth is 25 degrees.
